## 2026-07-18 (al) — the history charts are interactive now — hover a crosshair to read value + time, and it survives the live-morph

- **THE TENSION I CREATED, RESOLVED.** Two commits ago I made "these pages carry NO inline `<script>`" the REASON the live-morph is safe (innerHTML swap doesn't re-run scripts, so a JS-drawn chart would die on the first data update). An interactive chart needs JS — so a naive `onmouseover` per chart would break the moment the page morphed. Solved with EVENT DELEGATION: one `pointermove` handler on `document`, attached once OUTSIDE `#station`, so it survives every morph and simply re-targets whatever `.ichart` SVGs the fresh markup contains. No per-chart init to die.
- **NEAR-ZERO DATA COST.** The 9 equity/P&L curves were already server-rendered `<polyline>`s — the shape is ALREADY in the DOM. So the handler reads the polyline's own points for geometry and inverts a tiny 5-number scale per chart (`data-lo/hi` map y→value, `data-t0/t1` map index→time). Nothing per-point is embedded; the page grew by ~5 attributes per chart, not 9×420 points.
- **HTML overlay, not SVG.** The charts use `preserveAspectRatio="none"` (they stretch to full width), which would distort any SVG `<text>`. So the crosshair, the dot on the curve, and the tooltip are position:fixed HTML overlays the handler creates once and repositions — crisp at any width. Value formatted like `money()` (`+1,234.56`), time in UTC to match the page.
- **VERIFIED IN A REAL BROWSER, including the hard part.** Hovered a chart → correct readout ("+29.65 · 2026-07-15 16:39 UTC"), crosshair + dot + tooltip visible (screenshot). Then the make-or-break test: I reproduced the poller's morph EXACTLY (fetch page, swap `#station.innerHTML`), which DESTROYS the chart node — confirmed `chart_node_replaced: true` — and hover on the FRESH node STILL produced a correct tooltip. A per-chart init would have been dead there; delegation isn't. `node --check` on the combined JS; 9 charts render with valid scale attrs against the live DB. Guards green.
- **FOR THE NEXT PERSON:** the delegated handler keys on `.ichart` + the 5 data-attrs and reads a `<polyline>`. Any new chart that follows that shape is interactive for free. The morph-safety rule still stands for anything ELSE that needs JS — delegate from document, or live outside `#station`.

## 2026-07-18 (ak) — the live-station treatment now covers every dashboard page, applied once at the router

- **FOLLOW-ON to the main-page live station.** The five drill-down pages (/vitals health, /history, /learning, /periods, /market) still used full-page `<meta refresh>` (60–300s). They now live-update in place with the same heartbeat + morph, so the whole console is one operating station, not one live page and five reprinted photos.
- **DONE MECHANICALLY, not by hand-surgery.** A single `live_wrap(page)` helper transforms any page string: `<meta refresh>` → `<noscript>` fallback, inject heartbeat CSS, wrap the body in `<div id="station">` (morph target) closing before the footer so the footer + poller stay OUTSIDE it, drop the heartbeat in the footer + poller before `</body>`. Applied at the ONE dispatch site in do_GET (`live_wrap(render_vitals())` …), so every route gets identical treatment and none can be forgotten. Idempotent — it no-ops on the main page, which is already wired inline.
- **SAFE on these pages SPECIFICALLY because they carry NO inline `<script>`.** A morph replaces innerHTML and does not re-run scripts, so a page that drew a chart in JS would break after an update. Verified 0 `<script>`/`<canvas>` across all five before wiring; they render everything server-side. If a future drill-down adds a content script, it must re-init after morph or opt out of `live_wrap`.
- **VERIFIED:** `live_wrap` unit-tested (11 structural checks + idempotency + a footer-less edge case); all five pages rendered against the live DB and re-checked (DOM-balanced #station, noscript fallback, heartbeat present, poller outside the morph target); and the SERVED /vitals fetched through the real HTTP path confirmed correct. The client JS is byte-identical to the main page's, already browser-proven (in-place morph with a surviving sentinel, age ticker, red-OFFLINE on server loss). Guards green: deploy-coverage 0-orphan, born-dark 20/20.

## 2026-07-18 (aj) — the dashboard is now a live operating station: every item updates in place, no reload, and it shows when it goes blind

- **THE PROBLEM.** The whole dashboard refreshed by full-page `<meta http-equiv="refresh">` — 30s on the main page, up to 5 min elsewhere. That reloads the entire document: it flickers, drops your scroll position, and between reloads the page is a frozen snapshot with no indication of how old it is. Not an operating station — a periodically-reprinted photo.
- **THE FIX — in-place morph, gated by a cheap change signal.** New `/tick.json` (public, no auth — it is one timestamp) returns `GREATEST(max(bot_pnl.updated_at), max(bot_state.updated_at))` in ONE query. A vanilla-JS layer polls it every 5s and, ONLY when the version moves, fetches the page and swaps the `#station` container's innerHTML. Every card, total and organ updates at once; nothing flickers; scroll is kept. Because the expensive full render runs only when data actually changed, DB load is at or below the old blanket 30s meta-refresh, not above it.
- **IT SHOWS WHEN IT IS BLIND.** A heartbeat dot in the footer reads live (green) / reconnecting (amber) / **OFFLINE (red)** with a 1-second "updated Ns ago" ticker. An operating station must never present stale data as current — if the poller loses the server, the operator sees red within ~10s instead of numbers that quietly stopped moving.
- **FAIL-SAFE, THREE WAYS.** (1) `<noscript><meta refresh></noscript>` — a JS-off browser still refreshes. (2) The poller lives OUTSIDE `#station`, and innerHTML assignment does not run `<script>` tags, so a morph can never kill the loop driving it. (3) If `/tick.json` stays unreachable past the fallback window the page force-reloads — a broken poller degrades to the OLD behaviour, never a frozen screen. The admin "Manage bots" panel is deliberately OUTSIDE the morph target so its forms aren't swapped mid-interaction.
- **VERIFIED IN A REAL BROWSER, not by inspection.** Rendered against the live DB; drove the client with a mutating harness. Confirmed: green heartbeat + the morph token advancing (#22->#23) while an injected sentinel variable SURVIVED (proves in-place, no reload); the age ticker incrementing; and after killing the server, the heartbeat flipping to red "OFFLINE" with the age climbing. `node --check` on the extracted JS; `/tick.json` latency measured (sub-ms on Railway internal net; the 40s local full-render is 26 DB connects x proxy latency from my laptop, unchanged from today and fast on-box). Guards green: deploy-coverage 0-orphan, born-dark 20/20.
- **NOTE:** the code shipped in the prior commit (d67a91e) whose CHANGELOG write crashed on a regex bug before the entry landed — this is that entry. Scope: main page live now; /vitals + other drill-downs still meta-refresh (same one-line treatment extends to each; /vitals next).

This is the "labels that lie" hygiene, not a behaviour fix. `pnl_dashboard.py` byte-compiles.
- **Context: audited yesterday's additions against the dashboard registry — nothing else needs adding.** Every 17-Jul file that could publish a row (Ticket Taker go-live, the retirements) was already wired the same day; the rest were scripts / audit tools / Dockerfiles / a fills library / backtest artifacts, none of which publish a `bot_pnl` row.

## 2026-07-17 (ah) — the judge now refuses to spend real money on two arms running different code — and the "fix" I was authorized to make would have removed a real-money boundary

- **THE PLAN WAS TO PAIR THE ARMS. I MEASURED FIRST, AND DIDN'T.** The proposal (operator-approved) was to move each live bot's `-lshadow` CONTROL arm into its live sibling's service so one deploy moves both and drift becomes impossible. Then I read the env: `freqtrade-bots` and `funding-farmer-shadow` hold **ZERO key vars and no `REAL_MONEY_KILL`**; `trail-blazer-live` and `tide-rider-lighter-live` hold **2 key vars each + `REAL_MONEY_KILL=DISARMED_I_UNDERSTAND`**. The split is not an accident of layout — it is a **real-money isolation boundary**: the control arms are *physically incapable* of trading, whatever their code does. Pairing them would have moved each control book into a keyed, armed container, leaving a **`TT_VENUE` string** as the only thing between the control arm and real orders — trading a hard safety property for a soft one, to buy telemetry convenience. **Withdrawn.** The authorization rested on my framing, and my framing was wrong.
- **SO DRIFT STAYS POSSIBLE BY DESIGN — and is now HARMLESS instead.** `paired_eval` gained an ARM-DRIFT gate, checked BEFORE arm-skew: skew asks *"is the arm running the candidate?"*, drift asks the prior question *"are these two arms the same experiment at all?"*. Neither is a data-volume problem — **no window makes a drifted comparison valid**, so the judge holds, pages urgent, and does **not** age the candidate toward ABANDONED (it isn't the candidate's fault). Same fail-closed shape as ARM SKEW, for the same reason.
- **Build ids come from the arms' own bytes**, read off the SAME `rows` snapshot the bar is computed from — so the drift check and the numbers it gates can never describe different moments. Import is lazy + guarded: a dark sensor costs the judge nothing (it simply cannot claim drift).
- **Fail-safe toward SILENCE, deliberately.** Unknown is NOT drift — a missing stamp means "an arm hasn't published since the hook shipped", which is the normal state through any rollout **including this one**. A gate that fired on unknown would freeze the promotion queue every deploy. Mutation-tested both directions: disabling the gate (promotes across drifted arms) CAUGHT; firing on unknown (freezes the queue) CAUGHT.
- **THE FRONT DOOR IS PROVEN, not asserted.** The `bot_pnl_store` push in (ag) fired `Railway Redeploy` on its own and deployed **freqtrade-bots, pnl-dashboard, funding-carry, market-context**. Before tonight that exact push deployed *nothing* and looked identical — which is how 15 organs sat undeployed for 10 days. **11/18 bots now stamp their build**; the four family books all report `3673b404994a`, identical because they share a container. The two LIVE arms are unstamped (dispatch-only services, not yet restarted) and the sensor is correctly **silent** about them rather than crying drift — its own rollout is not its first false alarm.

## 2026-07-17 (ag) — the arms could drift in silence, and every label we might have used to notice was already lying

- **THE HAZARD.** Both live bots have a `-lshadow` CONTROL arm in a **different Railway service** (Farmer: `trail-blazer-live` vs `funding-farmer-shadow`; Taker: `tide-rider-lighter-live` vs the organ container) — four arms, four deploy clocks. `experiment_judge` **promotes to `live.funding.*`** when the shadow beats the live arm per-trade over ≥7d; `implementation_shortfall` reads that same gap as execution quality. Drifted arms put a strategy delta into both numbers and **neither organ can see it** — they compare OUTCOMES, the drift is in the INPUTS. MEASURED 17-Jul: the four agreed only because they happened to be deployed inside the same 23 minutes. Nothing would have said otherwise.
- **EVERY SELF-DESCRIBING LABEL IN THIS FLEET HAS LIED — one of them to me, tonight.** `RAILWAY_GIT_*` reads **0 on EVERY service, connected or not**; a session used it to conclude "not one of the 12 services is git-connected" and wrote it into memory. **FALSE — 9 services auto-deploy from main** and proved it by shipping `fc5c9a6` with *no Railway Redeploy run at all*. `railway status` meta.branch is blank for every `railway up`, so CI and a desk upload are indistinguishable. `lighter.__version__` reports "1.0.0", a release PyPI does not have, while the wheel signing real orders measured 1.1.2. **A sha256 of the source bytes cannot lie about being those bytes** — so the stamp is bytes, never a label. The right connectivity test is `railway status --json` → `activeDeployments[].meta.branch`.
- **IT LIVES IN `bot_pnl_store`, NOT ITS OWN MODULE — the born-dark guard made that call.** It was `build_id.py` for ten minutes, until the guard reported it **MISSING FROM 19 OF 20 IMAGES** (this file is universal; nothing COPYs that one), and `_stamp_build`'s `except` would have made every one a **silent** no-stamp. The fix is *not* 20 `COPY` lines — **that is a roster**, and tonight's lesson is that a roster nobody maintains is the control that already failed (`LIVE_FRESHNESS_LIMITS` rotted; the deploy path list rotted; the guard exists because `brain_stats` shipped to an image without it). Here **"can publish" == "can stamp", by construction** — a new bot is covered the day it ships and no Dockerfile can forget it.
- **The hashed set is FIXED and DECLARED** (entry module + `venues/` + `funding_basis` + this file), **not** "repo-local modules in `sys.modules`": a lazy or conditional import changes the module set without changing the code, so identical arms would report different ids and the detector would cry drift **forever**. The set must not depend on the execution path — the same reason `if dry_run` branches can't be compared across arms.
- **Verdict `arm-drift` follows the `xp-contaminated` precedent exactly**: report the number, REFUSE the verdict, name both builds. Ranked ABOVE it — an experiment is a difference we CHOSE and are measuring; drift is one nobody chose and nobody could see. Never phones (the streak only counts `live-slipping`).
- **FAIL-SAFE TOWARD SILENCE, deliberately opposite to `_xp_running`.** Unknown is NOT drift: a missing stamp means "an arm hasn't published since the hook shipped" — the normal state during any rollout, *including this one*. Firing there would make the feature's own deploy its first false alarm, which is how an alarm gets muted. Drift is claimed only on positive evidence: two stamps, both present, both different.
- **MUTATION-TESTED 6/6, none survived:** sensor blinded; drift stops outranking; unstamped read as drift; build id ignoring the entry module (two bots reading as one build); build id keyed on the ABSOLUTE path (**the first cut did exactly this** — `os.path.relpath` quietly returns `../../tmp/...` instead of raising into the basename fallback, so identical arms would have read as permanently drifted); and the stamp overwriting a caller's key.
- **A NEAR MISS WORTH RECORDING: my mutation harness lost its own backups to an `&&` short-circuit** — a failing selftest skipped the `cp` that was supposed to save the originals, so four mutations stayed in the working tree while the restores silently failed. Caught only because `git status` showed files I hadn't meant to change. The harness now holds the originals **in memory** and restores unconditionally. *The tool that verifies the fix needs the same paranoia as the fix.*
- Guards green: born-dark 20/20, deploy-coverage 0-orphan, sdk-pin. 15 organ selftests pass (`regime_oracle` needs pandas locally — fails identically at HEAD, not mine).

## 2026-07-17 (ah) — 🔮 Georgia's whole loss is ONE tag that is 63% of her trades — and cutting it only buys BREAK-EVEN

- **FOLLOW-UP TO (af), which asked "would a short fix Georgia?" and answered "the LONG side loses at zero friction — the payoff structure is the bug". THE PAYOFF DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG. I opened the ledger I had already written and it refuted me.** The payoff ratio is **0.970** (avg win **+1.3058%** vs avg loss **-1.3459%**) and **1.26 for the guilty tag itself** — its winners are 26% BIGGER than its losers. There is no capped-winner problem. Georgia needs a **50.8%** win rate and gets **49.7%**: she is **1.1pp from break-even**, not structurally broken. An "obviously right" mechanism I reasoned my way to, wrong on the data sitting next to it.
- **THE REAL BUG IS ONE TAG. `trend_breakout` is 62.7% of every trade she makes and it is the ENTIRE loss** — -$67.00 of the -$53.73 total (frictionless, 438d Lighter tape). **The other two modes are POSITIVE**: range_on +$12.11 (wr 61.4% vs a 59.9% break-even bar, +1.5pp), bounce_pullback +$1.16 (+0.3pp). trend_breakout runs **wr 42.3% against its own 44.3% break-even — a 2.0pp ENTRY-QUALITY shortfall**, not a payoff one.
- **WHY BELIEVE IT: A SECOND INSTRUMENT ALREADY SAID SO.** The brain's lens-forward grades put the `breakout` lens at **37.9% hit / -0.264% at 4h over n=2,445 LIVE scout tickets** — different data, different method, same verdict ([[taker-long-lenses-no-forward-edge]]). The backtest (n=2,294) and the live grader agree. **This is the only claim in this entry that does not rest on the bear tape.**
- **THE OBVIOUS FIX DOES NOT WORK. `trend_breakout` is the ONE mode with no macro-regime check** — `range_on` needs `regime_up`, `bounce_pullback` needs `NOT regime_up`, breakout needs **neither** — while V5's own header says the gate "forbids any buying while the long-term trend is down". 62.7% of her trades walk through that hole. **Measured: gating it recovers only $15 of $67 and STILL loses** (-$38.65). The mode is unprofitable in BOTH regimes, so it is not a gate hole — the signal has no edge on this venue.

      slip/side   BASELINE    breakout GATED   breakout DROPPED
      0.00 bps    - $53.73    - $38.65         + $11.21
      0.86 bps    - $85.83    - $54.38         +  $0.36
      5.00 bps    -$223.95    -$138.01         - $51.38

- **AND THE HONEST ENDING: THERE IS NO "MISSING WINNING FACTOR" IN THIS DESIGN.** Cut the one mode that loses and Georgia's **best case is +$0.36 over 438 DAYS** at the optimistic slippage — and negative at the assumed one. The surviving range edge is ~+$13 frictionless on a $1,000 book over 14 months (**~1.1%/yr GROSS**) — **below cash, and it does not survive friction**. Passive short-and-hold earned **+$324** on the same tape. The operator asked what she was missing; the measured answer is that there is nothing to find at 15m — the edge is real, tiny, and smaller than the cost of collecting it. Same shape as [[stock-leaders-retired-cant-clear-dd-gate]]: the only configuration that clears the bar earns below cash.
- **A SIDE EFFECT WORTH KNOWING: dropping breakout RAISES the trade count 1,362 -> 1,631.** The losing mode was **crowding the profitable ones out** of the 5 slots and the 2-per-hour throttle. A bad signal does not just lose its own money — it spends the budget the good signals needed.
- **REGIME-FITTING WARNING, STATED IN THE ENTRY BECAUSE THIS TAPE INVITES IT.** Every number above comes off a **61.5%-risk-off** tape, so *any* change that deletes long trades flatters itself here, and "both halves" cannot refute it (both halves fall — see §18). **The bear tape can price the bug; it cannot validate the fix.** The breakout verdict stands on the brain's independent live-ticket agreement, not on this replay. Only a bull regime settles the rest, and Lighter's ~27 non-crypto books are the only on-venue place to get one.
- **A LEAD, NOT A CLAIM — georgia's Lighter port may be running a timeframe her own strategy file abandoned.** `user_data/strategies/DayTraderV5Gated.py:63` moved **15m -> 1h on 3-Jul** ("NO 15m variant could ever be net-green... live: 155 trades -$21 @14% win; backtests: -19% to -25% every variant") and declares `timeframe = "1h"`. `lighter_family_bot.py:568` runs georgia at **`tf="15m"`**, while its sibling `crypto-intraday-15m` — the one *named* 15m — runs `tf="1h"`. The port header calls this deliberate and config-resolved, and the Kraken carriers are retired, so **I could not resolve whether this is intentional or inherited** (the reasoning behind the twin's change was Kraken's 0.52% fee, which does NOT transfer to zero-fee Lighter). Flagging, not fixing. **My measurement is agnostic to it and unchanged: at 15m, on Lighter's own tape, the ceiling is break-even.**
- **NO BOT LOGIC CHANGED.** georgia untouched; study only.

## 2026-07-17 (af) — "would a SHORT fix Georgia?" — the answer flips between two numbers nobody has ever measured, and the LONG side is broken at zero friction

- **THE QUESTION (operator): is a short position the winning factor 🔮 Georgia is missing? THE ANSWER: UNDECIDABLE — and that is the finding, not a dodge.** Replayed the SHIPPED `DayTraderGated` against Lighter's own whole 438d 15m tape (13 coins, 547k bars), plus an exactly-mirrored short sleeve. The sleeve's P&L **crosses zero between two slippage numbers that are BOTH unmeasured**: at 5.00bps/side (MY ASSUMPTION) short-only is **-$85.62** and the mirror **HURTS** (-$291.67 vs the long control's -$223.95, maxDD -31.8%, failing the 15% gate); at 0.86bps (the ShadowBroker's MODEL) short-only is **+$76.37** and the mirror **HELPS** (-$15.75 vs -$85.83, maxDD -9.5%, passing). Every conclusion inverts. `scripts/backtest_georgia_short_sleeve.py` carries that caveat in its **VERDICT SENTENCE**, not its small print — the exact lesson `backtest_funding_lighter.py` was WITHDRAWN for this same morning ("if a constant is load-bearing and unmeasured, it belongs in the verdict sentence or the verdict is not earned").
- **SO THE BLOCKER IS NOT MORE TAPE — IT IS 57 REAL FILLS NOBODY HAS READ.** This study cannot be finished from a backtest. Measuring Lighter's real per-book slippage off the LIVE Farmer/Taker fills decides its sign, and is worth more than any further replay. See [[real-fills-have-never-been-measured]].
- **WHAT IS SLIPPAGE-PROOF, AND IT IS THE REAL BUG: THE LONG SIDE LOSES AT *ZERO* FRICTION** — CONTROL is **-$53.73 at 0.00bps** (n=3,656, wr 49.7%). No execution improvement can rescue it because friction is not what kills it. **Direction was never the problem.** georgia is RIGHT more often than it is wrong and still bleeds (short tags range_off 62.5% wr, fade_rally 64.2% wr, both ~flat) — the signature of an ROI ladder that **CAPS winners (1.8% decaying to 0.5%) below what the 2.5xATR trailing stop risks**. A mirror inherits the SAME broken payoff on the other side. **Fix the payoff before adding sleeves.**
- **DOING NOTHING BEAT EVERY ARM AT EVERY SLIPPAGE.** Shorting the same 13 coins $50 each and holding the whole 438d earns **+$324, essentially slippage-INDEPENDENT** (+$323.65 @5bps -> +$324.02 @0bps) because it pays the spread ONCE, not 3,778 times. The best sleeve (+$101.58, frictionless) is 3.2x worse. **The bear was free to anyone who placed one trade and left; georgia's activity destroyed 69-126% of it.**
- **WHY: THE BEAR IS ON A TIMESCALE A 15m BOT CANNOT REACH.** BTC fell 32.9% over the tape — but that is only **0.0157% of drift inside georgia's MEDIAN 248-min hold**, against round-trip friction of 0.1000% @5bps (6.4x) and 0.0172% @0.86bps (1.1x). At every non-zero slippage, friction >= the drift available at that horizon. This is a fact about the TAPE, not the strategy.
- **THE NULL: it beats a coin flip and still loses.** Random-entry shorts (same coins/durations/stakes) lose -$147.83; the real sleeve's -$85.62 sits at the 92.5th pct unrestricted and **88.2nd with entries restricted to RISK-OFF bars** (which holds the regime gate constant so only entry TIMING is tested). Better than random, NOT significant at 95%, negative anyway.
- **FLEET-WIDE, AND BIGGER THAN GEORGIA: LIGHTER'S TAPE IS SINGLE-REGIME.** 438d, BTC -32.9%, risk-off **61.5%**, and **0% risk-on in Nov-25 / Dec-25 / Feb-26 / Jun-26**. So **"positive in both halves" is TOOTHLESS for anything directional** — both halves fall. Under the 17-Jul Lighter-only rule, **every directional backtest this fleet can run is a bear-market backtest**. CLAUDE.md prices that rule's cost as a LENGTH loss (14mo vs 2.7yr); the real cost is a **REGIME loss**, which no amount of length fixes. Added as agenda §18.
- **AND THE ON-VENUE FIX ALREADY EXISTS, UNUSED:** Lighter lists ~27 non-crypto books whose regimes are the opposite of crypto's — over their listed windows **WTI +23.0%, IWM +13.4%, QQQ +12.2%, NVDA +11.8%, SPY +8.1%** while BTC fell 32.9%. That is the bull tape the crypto-only universe cannot supply, WITHOUT leaving Lighter. **PREREQUISITE, or it actively harms: georgia's regime gate is BTC's 4h EMA50/200** — pointing it at SPY is incoherent (it reads risk-off 61.5% *while SPY rose 8.1%*, so it would suppress longs through the bull run and fire half-stake counter-trend instead). Fix the gate per-asset BEFORE widening the universe.
- **FUNDING IS IRRELEVANT AT THIS HOLDING PERIOD — measured, not assumed.** Shorts earned **+$5.12 across 3,778 trades** ($0.0014/trade). Flipping long->short swings funding by **<3c on a $50 clip at the 24h MAX hold** (per-coin, from the scout: SOL 0.0% .. ETH 10.5%). Funding is the Funding Farmer's whole edge because it holds for DAYS; georgia holds for HOURS. Do not reach for funding to rescue this.
- **TWO OF MY OWN CLAIMS, REFUTED BY MY OWN RUN.** (1) I predicted the sleeve would PRINT because the tape fell 32.9% — it **loses** at 5bps; the drift is unreachable at a 4h horizon. (2) I warned `fade_rally` (shorting rallies in risk-ON) was the dangerous mirror of the -99% V1Aggro disaster — it is the **only positive tag** (+$6.60, 64.2% wr); the loser is `trend_breakdown` (-$84.57 = 99% of the short loss, 43.7% wr), the *ungated* momentum mode. Both n are small and the tape is only 38.5% risk-on, so neither is settled — but the predictions did not hold.
- **AND A BUG IN MY OWN REPLAY THAT WOULD HAVE FAKED THE WHOLE STUDY.** The tape is in MILLISECONDS; `run()` treated `t` as seconds — so `age_min` was **1000x too large and EVERY trade exited after ONE BAR** on a bogus `max_hold_timeout`, while the 4-candle cooldown (3.6s) and the 2/hr throttle (3.6s window) **never bound at all**. The P&L, win rate and both-halves would all have looked plausible and been artifacts. **Nothing caught it but the trade count** — 7,817 vs the live book's ~3/day — the same shape as the sniper's `watching:201`. Fixed, plus a unit assertion at the tape boundary. Exit-mix now matches the live ledger: replay 67% trailing-stop / 22% roi vs live georgia (n=12) 67% / 33%.
- **THE MIRROR IS PROVEN, NOT ASSERTED.** Every rule is linear in price, so `--selftest` reflects real windows about a constant (c'=2P0-c, highs/lows swapped) and requires each long rule to map EXACTLY onto its short mirror: **0 mismatches over 2,258 real-tape cases**, 13 long entries exercised (so not vacuously green). It then **MUTATES the mirror 4 ways** (zone, cover, tick, EMA side) and requires each to be CAUGHT — all 4 are.
- **AND A DEFECT IN THIS STUDY'S OWN VERDICT FUNCTION, FIXED BEFORE IT COULD BE QUOTED.** `report_null` cut at a bare `p >= 95` — so at 0.86bps the SAME sleeve printed **"NO EDGE" at 94.2 (null A) and "EDGE: timing beats random" at 95.0 (null B)**. Two nulls of one strategy straddling the line IS the result — *borderline* — but a hard threshold made the FUNCTION the author of the verdict and left a quotable "EDGE" in the repo for a sleeve with no established edge. Now banded: >=97.5 EDGE / >=90 **BORDERLINE, explicitly NOT an edge** / >5 no edge, and a negative sleeve at a high percentile is told it is "beating a coin flip while losing money". Same disease as the assumed-5bps headline above: a decision resting on a number nobody pinned.
- **NO BOT LOGIC CHANGED.** georgia is untouched: the evidence does not support adding a sleeve, and the payoff-structure lead needs its own backtest. Study only. Guards run: born-dark **OK**; venue-purity clean for this file (Lighter-only by construction — 438d is the WHOLE tape, 600d returns 0 bars).

## 2026-07-17 (ae) — the one live roster nobody swept, and the guard I duplicated 23 minutes after a sibling built it

- **THE TICKET TAKER WAS INVISIBLE TO THE ONE WATCHDOG BUILT TO SURVIVE A REGION OUTAGE.** `market_context.LIVE_FRESHNESS_LIMITS` had rotted BOTH ways at once: it still named `crypto-trend-daily-lighter` (retired 17-Jul — a pruned row returns no age, and `if age is not None` skipped it in **silence**), and it never gained `lighter-ticket-taker-lighter`, the $34.67 real-money book that took that exact slot. Four organs carry a live cohort; three were swept on 17-Jul and this was the fourth. `fleet_respiration.py:57` is the receipt: that sweep **examined this dict**, wrote down that its semantics differ ("treats it as no-evidence"), and moved on. Unrefuted is not checked.
- **FIXED BY DELETING THE ROSTER.** It is now DERIVED — `extra->>'venue' = 'lighter_live'` — exactly as `fleet_watchdog_svc.py:137` does it, the only live-cohort reader that survived the 17-Jul swap untouched *because it never hardcoded one*. A name cannot rot if nobody maintains it. `LIVE_CADENCE_SEC` keeps per-bot limits; an **unlisted** live row is still watched at `DEFAULT_LIVE_LIMIT` — a late page, never no page. Discovery reads the LAST PUBLISHED row, which survives the bot's death, so the case this organ exists for is the case discovery still catches. **Verified in production**: `live-freshness: 2 live row(s) watched (lighter-ticket-taker-lighter, perps-funding-lighter-lighter)`.
- **`fleet-alerts` was the ONLY bot_state key with no `updated`/`ttl_sec`** — so the board could not gate it, and `_fresh()` would have failed CLOSED and *blinded* the board rather than protecting it. Stamped at the writer (`save_alerts`, ttl 8h = the 6h coin-quality cadence + slack). `fleet_immune` now **preserves** that stamp on prune instead of re-minting it: filtration is content-only, and re-stamping would report on the janitor, not the producer (the `fleet_regen` rule).
- **AN EMPTY BOARD IS NOT EVIDENCE OF A HEALTHY FLEET** — it read identically to a dead `market_context`. `board:alerts-feed-dark` (warn) says the bloodstream stopped. It does **not** blank `fa`: every consumer already windows on each alert's own `ts` (down-reflex 6h, veto-flap 7d, score decay 12h), so blanking would discard valid data and buy nothing. Cadence-safe by fixture — 6h is *perfect health* and must stay silent, or the check fires on cadence and gets muted within a day. **Unstamped = info, not warn**: market-context deploys on its own clock, and an alerting organ whose first act is a false page is an organ you learn to ignore.
- **A CLAIM OF MINE, WITHDRAWN.** I reported that a stale `live-shadow-gap` could drive a 0.75 down-scale on real money. **It cannot** — `synthesize_live` already checks each alert's own `max(ts, last_seen)` against a 6h bar, tighter than any feed TTL, and a dead producer writes no fresh timestamps. The hazard was not reachable; the silence was the real defect.
- **proprioception's gapscout lane had NO episode floor** — a bare `any()`, the only lane skipping `MIN_EPISODES` while the module docstring advertised "floors n>=2" for all of them. It published **n=1 HELPING** for a **retired** bot, and the board carried it into the 21-Jul review as "the widening is paying". Its selftest had **pinned the missing floor as the contract**, one line above the live lane's own "a single episode never verdicts (floor)" — the same file asserting opposite rules for the same question. Verified in production: both verdicts flipped `helping -> neutral`, `helping: 0`, and the two zombie board items dropped off.
- **`bot_learn._trade_side` returned None for EVERY lens-tagged row — 100% of the LIVE Ticket Taker's closes.** `split_reason` moves the direction prefix OUT of `exit_reason` and INTO `enter_tag` *before* this function runs, so source 2 hunted a prefix that had already moved into the field source 1 only equality-checked. Both missed. The fixture hand-built `{"exit_reason": "short-divergence_tp"}` — **a shape production never delivers** — so it stayed green throughout. It now drives the real `split_reason`. Mutation-tested: restore the bug, 3 checks fail; the OLD fixtures stay green, which is exactly why it survived.
- **THE BOARD NOTIFIED A WARN/ACTION EXACTLY ONCE, AT ONSET, AND NEVER AGAIN** — `escalated` reads `trend`, and every board item's trend is the literal `"steady"`, so it was False **by construction** and the gate collapsed to `is_new`. `NOTIFY_GAP_H` was unreachable for the entire board-authored half. Added `worsened` (severity climbed) + `sustained` (`RENOTIFY_H`=12h, restrict-side only; re-paging good news is noise). **The score literals are DELIBERATE and now say so** rather than inviting a wrong fix: a level-triggered reading must not decay, and a cross-feed join is corroborated by construction. Ranking untouched.
- **I DUPLICATED A SIBLING'S GUARD, 23 MINUTES LATE — and mine shipped a FALSE-OK.** `scripts/audit_deploy_coverage.py` landed 21:42; I wrote `audit_deploy_filter.py` at 22:05, and **it was already an ancestor of my own commit**. I fetched and never read what arrived — and I never read or wrote THIS FILE, which exists precisely so parallel sessions don't do that. Mine was also broken: **delete the freqtrade-bots matcher entirely and it still printed "OK — all 19 organs gate a deploy"**, because `freqtrade_matcher()` returns None on a regex miss and `main()` said `elif matcher is not None and ...` — fail-OPEN. My selftest only drove the parser on well-formed input, never `main()` with a matcher it couldn't parse. **WITHDRAWN** rather than fixed: two guards over one contract is two things to keep in sync, and the weaker one's false-OK would have been believed.
- **EVERY ORGAN CAN NOW DEPLOY.** The push-path filter was last extended 2026-07-07; everything built since had **no deploy path** — a fix merged, went green, and never reached the container. Two lists gate one deploy (`paths:` decides if the job RUNS, the per-service `grep -qE` decides WHAT ships); being in one but not the other deploys nothing while looking green. Both fixed, plus a `market-context` block that did not exist. The last 4 came from the sibling's guard, which reads each service's real file set from its **Dockerfile**: `fleet_watchdog_svc.py` -> pnl-dashboard (**the dashboard's own watchdog**), `dashboard_server.py`/`paper_broker.py` -> freqtrade-bots, `paper_broker.py` **also** -> funding-carry (one file, two images — a per-organ list cannot express that), and `funding_basis.py`, already live in `paths:` but absent from funding-carry's grep. - **TWO CLAUDE.md CLAIMS REFUTED:** the PAT **can** push workflow files (this entry's workflow fix went straight to main; "workflow-file edits go via the GitHub web editor" is false), and the born-dark guard **is** already wired into CI (`changelog-check.yml`), not a standing follow-up.

## 2026-07-17 (ad) — the taker's id filter was a CLIFF, not a filter: one venue change from measuring NOTHING

- **THE REGRESSION THE ID INTRODUCED.** `venues/lighter_client.py:624-630` matches the client id HARD — a mismatch `continue`s, with no fall back to the `(is_ask, since_ts)` heuristic. So threading `client_order_index` made the taker's fill read exact on the happy path and **strictly worse than the read it replaced on the unhappy one**: if Lighter ever stops echoing the id back as `ask_client_id`/`bid_client_id`, the taker goes from an approximate read to **`no-match:both` — no read at all**, silently. A measurement upgrade whose failure mode is *less measurement than before* is not an upgrade; it is a cliff with a good day.
- **AND THE ID ROUND TRIP IS UNPROVEN IN PRODUCTION.** The taker's only two live orders (STRC 09:08:21Z, 1000BONK 09:13:30Z) were placed an **hour before** the fill-read code landed (a710401, 10:15Z). **No `venue_orders` row has ever carried `measured`/`fill_src`** — so "the id comes back" is an assumption the fleet has never once observed. The fallback is what makes that assumption cost precision instead of the whole reading.
- **FIXED by sharing the Farmer's read, not copying it** — `venues/fills.py`, the same consolidation as `venues/safety.py::open_notional`. Id first; on a **`no-match` only**, one retry without it, labelled `(id-miss: ...)`. Never on `skipped:budget` / `auth-failed` / `api-error` — those mean the tape was never read, so a retry spends the governor's telemetry reserve to fail identically, against lighter_client's explicit rule that telemetry must never make the next `market_open` queue behind it.
- **WHY NOT IN `_our_fills`, where the filter actually lives — two reasons, both fatal:**
  - **The label would lie.** `last_fill_detail` derives its reason from whether an id was *supplied*, not whether it *matched* (`"trades" if client_id is not None else "trades(approx)"`, :700). A fallback inside `_our_fills` returns a blend still labelled `trades` = exact, and `measured_from_reason` stamps it **True**. The fabrication would sit one layer below every test that could catch it.
  - **It would preempt the better tape.** `last_fill_detail` reads the auth'd `trades` (10 rows) then the public `recentTrades` (100 rows). An id-miss on tape 1 currently escalates to tape 2 **with the id**; a fallback inside `_our_fills` blends on tape 1 and returns, never reaching the wider tape where the exact match is more likely. The correct order is id-on-both-tapes, **then** heuristic-on-both — expressible only *above* `last_fill_detail`.
- **A SECOND DEFECT, found in the same read and worse than the first: `measured` was HARD-CODED TRUE.** `_real_fill` returned `real, True, reason` for **any** price the venue produced, ignoring the reason string that says whether the venue NAMED the fill or guessed it. An id-less read is a 180s same-side VWAP labelled `trades(approx)` — and it would have been recorded as an exact measurement. **A fabrication wearing a `measured: true` stamp is worse than a null**, because a null is honest about itself. Now derived from the venue layer's own label (`measured_from_reason`), so the two cannot drift.
- **THE DRIFT WAS REAL AND ABOUT TO BECOME REACHABLE.** The two copies disagreed on one rule: an **unmeasured** leg with `d != f` — the taker returned None, the Farmer returned the bps. Unreachable while every read was exact-or-nothing (unmeasured meant no price, so `d == f` always held). **`read_fill`'s fallback is exactly what makes it reachable** — an approx read is the first thing in the fleet's history to carry a real price AND `measured=False`. The Farmer's rule would have fed a **-49.99 bps blend** into `implementation_shortfall._fetch_order_slip`, which does an unfiltered `AVG(slippage_bps)` and reads no `measured` flag — one contaminated row is indistinguishable from a clean one in the live-vs-shadow execution verdict. **Unified on the stricter rule** (the taker's): record the blend as `px_fill`, record NO slippage from it.
- **THE TELEMETRY/BEHAVIOUR BOUNDARY — the taker is NOT the Farmer here, and this is the load-bearing difference.** The Farmer's `_real_exit` is pure forensics: the position is already flat when it runs. The taker's **entry** read becomes `meta[sym]["entry"]`, which the **stop and TP hang off** — it decides when real money closes. So the fallback's price reaches the **ledger only**: `entry_px = _fill_px if _meas else mark`. **Behaviour-identical on every path production can reach today** (an exact match still feeds meta the real fill; no-read still feeds it the mark); only the id-miss path is touched, and it yields the mark — which is what it yields today anyway. The blend is probably a better entry estimate than the decision mark; **"probably" is not a bar this bot clears with real money on the book.** The ledger records it, names it, and a later change can promote it on evidence.
- **NOT CONSOLIDATED, deliberately, and the brief's premise was half wrong.** `_real_exit`/`_slip_bps_of` are **not** four-way duplicated: `_slip_bps_of` exists in **two** bots (Farmer + taker), which are exactly the two that publish `slippage_bps`. `lighter_trend_bot`/`lighter_perp_sniper` have a *same-named, different-contract* function — legacy `last_fill`, no id, no `measured`, and **neither file writes a `venue_orders` row at all**. Their read is an exit PRICE for P&L, not execution telemetry. Folding them in would change two more bots' ledgers for no measurement gain. Left alone.
- **MUTATION-TESTED — 6 mutations, each RED on its own assertion:** revert the fallback to the hard filter (px_fill collapses to the decision), restore `measured=True` (a blend stamped as measured), retry on any failure rather than `no-match` (burns the reserve), the Farmer's old slip rule (records the -49.99 blend), **and both directions of the behaviour boundary** — an unmeasured blend reaching `meta['entry']`, and over-correcting so a *measured* fill can't. The `_StubVenue` grew `echo_ids=False` to model the actual failure — Lighter stops echoing ids, tape readable, our id absent — using lighter_client's **real** reason strings, because a paraphrased fixture tests nothing.
- **A NEAR MISS WORTH RECORDING: I mutation-tested the wrong suite first.** All four originals came back "caught" — by the **Farmer's** suite, while the taker's stayed green — because check 12 lives in `_selftest_live()` and `--selftest` never runs it. `M2 (measured=True)` **SURVIVED** and exposed it. Had the Farmer not shared the code, four green mutations would have read as proof. *Same evening, fourth instance of the same shape: the fixture ran, the wiring did not.*
- **Verification:** taker `--selftest` + `--selftest-live` (13 checks), Farmer, replay, shortfall, trend, sniper, carry, safety, lighter_client (as a module) all green; born-dark OK across 20 images **and negative-tested for this exact import shape** (`venues.fills` imported by an image lacking `COPY venues/` → correctly named); venue-purity unchanged (6 pre-existing, none mine); `audit_sdk_pin` green. **Telemetry + ledger only: no change to what any bot sends** — `market_open`/`market_close`/sizing/rails/`meta['entry']` untouched on every reachable path.
- **DOC ROT FOUND EN ROUTE (not fixed here — shared file):** CLAUDE.md still says the born-dark guard is "NOT wired into CI (the PAT lacks workflow scope) ... the standing follow-up". **It was wired in `ce446c7`** and runs on every push/PR. The rule's own postmortem says a control that relies on remembering is the one that already failed — the doc telling the next reader to go do it by hand is that failure mode's second act.

- **THE FIX FROM (ab) IS LIVE AND IT WORKED — three lines that were invisible an hour ago**, off the LIVE taker's own boot at 11:17Z:

True when written; **a LIE the moment (aa) pinned it** — and it shipped that lie to the live taker's boot log tonight, where the next reader would have taken it as current. A log line cannot know the repo's state. It now states only the FACT it measured: the version. Same class as the comment-honesty fixes, and a reminder that **a diagnostic decays like any other claim** — the ones that assert context rot fastest.

## 2026-07-17 (ab) — the live taker was BLIND to its own venue layer: no signer, no equity-guard REJECTs, no governor
- **`lighter_ticket_taker.py` configured NO logging, so Python dropped every `log.info` from `venues/`.** The LIVE real-money bot could not see: its **signer banner** (and therefore its lighter-sdk version), **EQUITY GUARD REJECTs**, governor **429/punish**, ws-degraded. Only WARNING+ ever surfaced — via the handler of last resort — which is exactly why `Unclosed client session` was visible and nothing else was.
- **THE ASYMMETRY IS WHAT EXPOSED IT, and it is worth recording as the detection method.** (z) added a signer banner naming the wheel that signs real orders. The **funding bot printed it on its first boot**; the **taker printed nothing**. Two bots, same venue layer, one answer — the difference was not the banner, it was that one bot could hear its own dependencies. **A diagnostic that cannot be seen is not a diagnostic**, and the only reason the SDK got measured at all is that the *other* bot happened to configure logging.
- **FIXED — `_setup_logging()`, called from `main()`, never at import.** FOUR modules import this file (`lighter_ticket_replay`, `lighter_scout_tuner`, `strategy_incubator`, `fleet_proprioception`) and **none of them configure logging**, so an import-time `basicConfig` would silently seize the root logger for all four inside the shared freqtrade-bots container. Same reason the `TT_VENUE` gate lives inside `main()`. Idempotent (`basicConfig` no-ops when the root has handlers, so a caller keeps its own setup); `_ctx` — the offline test path — is left alone so the selftests' captured stdout stays clean. Level via `TT_LOG_LEVEL`, default INFO.
- **PINNED BOTH WAYS, because this fix has two ways to be wrong:** silent (no handler → the original bug) and invasive (hijacking four importers). Test 13 asserts the venue logger is INFO-enabled after setup, that merely importing adds **no** handler, that the `_ctx` path configures nothing, and that a second call is a no-op. Verified live: all four importers still see **0 root handlers**.
- **AND THE MUTATION CAUGHT ME A THIRD TIME — same trap, three times in one evening.** My fixture called `_setup_logging()` DIRECTLY, so it proved the FUNCTION worked and said nothing about `main()` calling it — and a helper nobody calls **is the original bug**. **M14 (delete the call site) PASSED.** This is verbatim `_bars` in `funding_carry_bot` and `_slip_bps_of`'s `measured` earlier tonight: **test the helper, forget the wiring.** Fixed with the same source-level call-site assertion; M14/M15/M16 now all fail correctly. The pattern is now three-for-three and belongs in the review: *a fixture that constructs its own inputs proves the arithmetic, never the wiring.*
- **Demonstrated, not asserted:** before `_setup_logging`, `venues.lighter`'s `log.info("lighter signer ready ... lighter-sdk 1.1.2")` printed **nothing**; after, it prints — along with `WARNING venues.lighter equity guard REJECT`. That is the bug and the fix in two lines of output.
- **Verification:** 7 selftests + `--selftest-live` (13 checks) green; born-dark OK across 20 images; `audit_sdk_pin` green; all four importers import with 0 root handlers; deployed to the LIVE taker from a clean `origin/main` worktree per (aa), both real positions intact, 0 orders across the restart.

## 2026-07-17 (aa) — the taker's telemetry IS LIVE, and the signer's wheel is MEASURED (1.1.2) and PINNED

What I had "measured" was a transient mid-write state in a shared tree, and I had let it stand as a fact for an hour. **A working tree is not a claim about anything except this second.**
- **🎫 THE LIVE TAKER NOW RUNS TONIGHT'S TELEMETRY.** Deployed 21:02 Sydney; booted clean; **both real positions intact** (STRC + 1000BONK, 2/2, $34.69); zero orders across the restart. - **PINNED to `==1.1.2` — a NO-OP for the running fleet, which is the whole point.** It is exactly what the live Farmer is measured to run, so nothing moves today and the drift stops. **Pinning to the docs' "verified" 1.1.1 would have DOWNGRADED a live signer that is placing orders fine** — the trap that made this the operator's call until the number existed. Pinned in **BOTH** install sites (`requirements.txt` AND `Dockerfile.freqtrade`, which names the dep by hand) — one alone is a decorative pin.
- **NEW GUARD + CI: `scripts/audit_sdk_pin.py`.** A floating spec on a wheel that signs real orders now fails at author time and on every push. **This is the sibling control for the layer `audit_image_imports` is blind to by construction** — its own docstring says it "verified the FILE and not the WHEEL", which is 17-Jul (m), the hour the taker crash-looped on a missing `lighter-sdk`. **Negative-tested against the REAL pre-fix state**: restore `>=1.1.1` in either file and it fails, naming the line. A guard that only passes on a clean repo is the vacuous bar.

Only WARNING+ reaches stderr via the handler of last resort (which is why `Unclosed client session` shows and nothing else does). The funding bot configures logging and printed the wheel on its first boot. **Not fixed here** — `lighter_ticket_taker.py` was a sibling's active surface all evening and I have already tangled with it once tonight; it is a one-line `basicConfig` and it belongs to whoever holds that file next.
- **Verification:** clean-tree gate (taker `--selftest` + `--selftest-live`, funding/trend/carry/basis, lighter_client, born-dark 20 images) green **inside the deployed artifact**; live taker marker-grepped post-deploy (2 positions, 0 orders); Farmer's 4 positions unchanged across its own restart; `audit_sdk_pin` + its selftest green and negative-tested; gate0 kept == main.

## 2026-07-17 (z) — gate0 FF'd (the rollback hazard is closed, live Farmer survived it clean); the SDK is MEASURED before it is pinned
- **F1 CLOSED — gate0 fast-forwarded `eecd806` -> `5b5b97f`.** Done as a **PURE REF-PUSH** (`git push origin origin/main:refs/heads/claude/lighter-gate0`), which pushes the REMOTE ref and **never reads the working tree** — mandatory tonight, because a concurrent session has half-finished `load_state_checked` work sitting in `lighter_ticket_taker.py` that currently fails `--selftest-live`. A `git push` from the checkout would have shipped it.
- **The hazard it removes, restated because my first read of it was BACKWARDS:** `tide-rider-lighter-live` (the taker) is **not git-connected** — gate0 could never roll it back. **`trail-blazer-live` — the LIVE Funding Farmer — TRACKS gate0**, so a redeploy for any reason would have reverted the real-money bot to this morning's code. Leaving gate0 stale was the risk; the ff was the fix. gate0 now carries the taker's image, `allowed_lenses` (0 -> **11**), and the lighter-sdk install.
- **SAFETY CHECK THAT MATTERED: `origin/main` carries the RETRACTION, not the guard.** Verified before pushing — `min_size_error`'s call site is **absent** from what deploys and the withdrawal note is present. Had (y) not landed first, this ff would have deployed the order-blocking guard straight onto the live Farmer's ZEC/HYPE entries. The retraction was 40 minutes ahead of the thing that would have shipped it.
- **THE LIVE FARMER RESTARTED AND CAME BACK CLEAN — verified against a pre-ff snapshot, not assumed.** Snapshot: `$65.90, 4 open, {ETH:S, XAU:S, ZEC:S, SNDK:S}`. Post-ff: `scan ok | 214 perps | held: ETH(S), XAU(S), ZEC(S), SNDK(S) | realized $+6.14`, **all four positions intact**, `$65.92`. **ZERO real orders fired across the restart** (the 11-Jul lesson: a redeploy wiped a memory-only halt and HYPE re-bought 37s after boot). The taker is untouched at `$34.62, 2 open`. The documented cost was paid as designed: the Farmer's slope gate "fails open for ~1h after restart".
- **🔴 THE SDK IS NOT PINNED, AND I REFUSE TO PIN IT BY INFERENCE.** `requirements.txt:7` is `lighter-sdk>=1.1.1`; **1.1.2 shipped 2026-07-10**; every image built since resolves to PyPI's latest on build day. **That wheel IS the signer** (`SignerClient`, `create_market_order`, the Go binary, the nonce manager) — a build-time `pip install` can silently swap the code that moves real money, and the born-dark guard **cannot see a pip dep** (its own docstring says so).
- **BUT NOBODY HAS MEASURED WHICH VERSION IS DEPLOYED.** The build logs are cached/truncated; `lighter.__version__` reports a stale `"1.0.0"` — **a release PyPI does not even have** (0.1.5 -> 1.0.1), so it lies to anyone who checks the obvious way (`importlib.metadata` is the truth). Pinning to `1.1.1` DOWNGRADES a live signer that is placing orders fine; pinning to `1.1.2` pins a version nobody reviewed. **Both move the signer under a bot holding real positions on a guess.** That is precisely the mistake (y) retracted an hour earlier, and I am not making it twice in one evening.
- **SO: MEASURE FIRST.** `_init_signer` now names its own wheel at every boot — `lighter signer ready (account N, key index 4) | lighter-sdk X.Y.Z (requirements pins >=1.1.1 — UNPINNED; this wheel signs real orders)`. The next deploy turns "which SDK signs our orders?" from an argument into a **grep**, and the pin becomes a measured decision. Fail-safe (`unknown` on any error), zero risk, and it is the same move as the fill telemetry: **you cannot fix what you have not measured, so make the unknown observable and stop guessing.**
- **NOT DONE, and it must not be: `railway up` on the live taker.** It is the only way to deploy tonight's telemetry fix (that service is not git-connected, so the ff does not reach it) — and **`railway up` uploads the LOCAL DIRECTORY**, which right now contains the concurrent session's half-finished `load_state_checked`. It `SystemExit`s every cycle on a state read. Deploying it would take the live taker down **while it holds two real positions**, with nothing managing their stop or max-hold. **Wait for that session to land its work.**
- **Verification:** ff pre-flighted as a clean ancestor; guard-absence confirmed in the pushed tree; live Farmer's 4 positions + taker's 2 reconciled against the snapshot; 0 orders across the restart; lighter_client selftest green; born-dark OK across 20 images.

## 2026-07-17 (y) — WITHDRAWN: my "$10 is the venue floor" was an ASSUMPTION, and the fleet's own ledger refuted it in one query
- **(x) IS WRONG AND THIS RETRACTS IT. The guard I shipped would have BLOCKED LIVE REAL-MONEY ORDERS.** I found `min_base_amount` / `min_quote_amount` in the market list, **assumed they were enforced**, derived "$10 is a HARD FLOOR on all 201 books; there is no $5 clip", told the operator that as fact, and shipped a `min_size_error` guard into the shared real-money client. Every load-bearing word of it was unmeasured.
- **REFUTED, both halves, by `venue_orders` — the rows were sitting there the whole time.**
  - **`min_base_amount` is NOT enforced for market orders: 16 of 56 real orders were BELOW it and the venue ACCEPTED every one.** Including the **LIVE Funding Farmer's ZEC — 0.037358 against a min_base of 0.1, sent 17-Jul 10:36, while I was writing the guard that would have refused it** — plus its HYPE (0.33 vs 0.5) and Tide Rider's TRX (38.8 vs 40).
  - **`min_quote_amount` is NOT a $10 floor: the smallest real order ever sent is $5.00** — four of them (`perps-donchian-breakout-lighter`, HYPE + kBONK, 10/11-Jul) — **and they FILLED** (67.0896 / 66.3824 / 0.003998 / 0.004142).
- **THE BLAST RADIUS I NEARLY SHIPPED.** `min_size_error` sat in `market_open` in `venues/lighter_client.py` — the **shared** surface both live bots use. It was committed and pushed (`115d470`). The gate0 fast-forward **I recommended in the same breath** would have deployed it to `trail-blazer-live` and silently blocked the live Funding Farmer's ZEC and HYPE entries. The guard is REMOVED; `market_open` is back to `base <= 0` and the venue is the authority again, as it always was.
- **WHAT THOSE FIELDS ACTUALLY GATE IS UNKNOWN** (limit orders? resting orders? UI display?). **Unknown is the honest answer.** The withdrawal note is left in the file so nobody rebuilds this, with the bar for ever doing so: a fixture proving the venue actually rejects a sub-minimum MARKET order.
- **THE OPERATOR'S ANSWER IS THEREFORE THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT I GAVE: the clip CAN go below $10.** $5 orders demonstrably fill on this venue. `TT_CLIP_MIN/MAX=10` is still a fine choice and still halves the bet — but **it is a choice, not a floor**, and if you want $5 the evidence says $5 works. The rest of (x) stands and is unaffected: the $10-vs-$15 trade-off framing was fiction, but the **arithmetic** was not — two stops at $15 lose $0.90 (2.6% of the book), the real 2-goes exposure is the $2 daily rail, and the genuine tail is a gap through the stop on an illiquid book.
- **THE SELFTEST NOW PINS THE REFUTATION, not my fiction.** Its fixtures are the REAL orders the venue ACCEPTED (ZEC 0.037358, HYPE 0.33, TRX 38.8, and the two $5 fills) — any future "minimum order" guard must let all of them through or it is wrong the same way. `venues/lighter_client.py` still gets its first-ever selftest out of this; only its subject changed.
- **AND THE REWRITE CAUGHT A SECOND, REAL DEFECT OF MINE.** `_our_fills` matched on `client_id` **alone**, dropping the account-ownership check. The client id is `int(time.time()*1000) % 2**48` — **timestamp-derived, unique per ACCOUNT, not globally** — so two accounts ordering in the same millisecond collide and I would have read **a stranger's fill as ours**. Ownership is now checked on BOTH paths (the id NARROWS, it never authorises), with a collision fixture that fails without it.
- **THE LESSON IS THE ONE THIS ENTIRE DAY WAS ABOUT, and I walked into it anyway.** I spent the session documenting "an unmeasured constant makes a false verdict" — the 8x basis, the assumed 5bps slippage, the borrowed HL backtests — and then read a field NAME, believed it, and handed the operator a confident floor. **The refutation cost one query against the outcome ledger.** The tell was available and free: the fleet had already sent $5 orders and they had already filled. **Ask what MEASURED the number — including, especially, when the number is your own.**
- **Verification:** guard removed from `market_open`; lighter_client selftest green on the refutation + the collision fixture; taker `--selftest` + `--selftest-live` green; funding bot + trend bot green; born-dark OK across 20 images.

## 2026-07-17 (x) — "lower each bet": the venue says $10 is the FLOOR, and a $15 clip has been silently dropping AAPL
- **THE ANSWER TO "LOWER THE BET" IS BOUNDED BY THE VENUE, and the bound is the finding.** MEASURED across all **201 active Lighter books**: every one sets `min_quote_amount = **$10**`. So **$10 is a HARD FLOOR** — a clip below it is refused on **201/201 books** and the taker can trade NOTHING. There is no $5 option. `$15 -> below the floor on 6/201 (3%)`, `$10 -> 25/201 (12%)`, `$8/$5/$4 -> 201/201 (100%)`.
- **AND THE $15 CLIP IS ALREADY SILENTLY DROPPING TICKETS.** 6 books set a `min_base_amount` worth MORE than $15: **AAPL $16.67, ZEC $53.53, VVV $30.80, HYPE $30.32, SKHYNIXUSD $23.48, XRP $21.69**. **AAPL is a LIVE divergence ticket right now.** The venue rejects a sub-minimum order with an opaque `err`, so `market_open` raised a generic "order failed", the taker logged `open AAPL failed` and moved on — a ticket dropped with no stated cause. Nobody could have known which books the clip had quietly excluded.
- **`min_size_error()` — pure, so it could be TESTED.** It names the cause: *"the CLIP is too small for this book, not a venue fault"*. It also saves the **WEIGHT_ORDER_TX (6 governor tokens)** of an order the venue was always going to reject. **OPEN ONLY — `market_close` must NEVER consult it**, because refusing to close a sub-minimum position would **STRAND** it, which is the exact failure class this fleet spent 17-Jul removing.
- **`venues/lighter_client.py` HAD NO SELFTEST — not one, ever.** A guard buried in `market_open` would have shipped unexercised, which is the trap that cost the fleet all day. First selftest added; scope stated honestly (the pure surfaces, not the loop/signer). **The REGRESSION FIXTURE is both of the taker's REAL fills** — STRC 0.175 @ 85.629 and 1000BONK 4580 @ 0.003275 must PASS. A guard that blocks a real fill is wrong, and that is the only way to know.
- **Mutation-tested, and the BOUNDARY case is why it was worth doing.** Dropping the min_base check and dropping the $10 floor are both caught. But `<` -> `<=` — an off-by-one that refuses an order sitting EXACTLY on the minimum, shaving the edge off every book — **passed**, because no fixture sat on the boundary. Added: `size == min_base` and `notional == min_quote` must both be LEGAL. Now caught. (Undeclared minimums fail-OPEN: the venue stays the authority; this only EXPLAINS.)
- **"MAKE SURE IT DEFAULTS TO LIGHTER" — already true, and the taker cannot leave Lighter by any path. VERIFIED, not asserted:** `TT_VENUE` defaults to `lighter_shadow` (:116); `TT_MODES = (lighter_paper, lighter_shadow, lighter_live)` — **all three are Lighter, there is no other venue in its vocabulary**; **zero** reads of the shared `$VENUE`; **zero** `hyperliquid`/`hl_paper` references in the whole file. **Nothing changed** — and deliberately so: `main()` REQUIRES `TT_VENUE` explicitly rather than defaulting, which is (n)'s fix ("identity must never be inherited" — its `lighter_shadow` id is a row another service already publishes; a default there is two writers, one row, no page). Reversing that to "default it" would rebuild the exact hole today closed twice.
- **THE STRUCTURAL FACT THE OPERATOR SHOULD SEE, and it is a go-live input:** on a **$34.63** book the venue's $10 minimum leaves the taker **almost no sizing freedom** — the smallest legal bet is **29% of the book**, and 2 slots at the floor is **58%**. `vol_clip`'s constant-risk sizer (`RISK_USD/adverse`, default $1.50) is **inert here**: the live service pins `TT_CLIP_MIN=TT_CLIP_MAX=15`, collapsing the bounds so every book gets a flat $15 regardless of volatility. You cannot risk-size a book this small on this venue.
- **THE ARITHMETIC ON "lose it in 2 goes", stated plainly because it does not support the fear:** at a $15 clip with the live `TT_SL=-3%`, one stop loses **$0.45** and two lose **$0.90** — **2.6% of the book, not the book**. The real 2-goes exposure is the **daily-loss rail** (`LIGHTER_MAX_DAILY_LOSS=$2`): two bad trades trip it and the bot halts for the day. Lowering the clip to $10 cuts a stop to $0.30 and pushes the rail further away — which is a good reason to do it, just not the stated one. The genuine tail is a **gap through the stop on an illiquid book** (STRC's half-spread is 41.6bps), which no clip size removes and a smaller clip does bound.
- **NOT APPLIED BY ME — env on a LIVE real-money service is the operator's.** The change is `TT_CLIP_MIN=10`, `TT_CLIP_MAX=10`, `LIGHTER_TICKET_TAKER_MAX_NOTIONAL=20` on `tide-rider-lighter-live`. Verified the cap rail uses `<=` (`venues/safety.py:93`), so 2 x $10 = $20 fits a $20 cap exactly and the second slot is NOT blocked. It affects **new entries only** — the two open positions carry their $15 clips in `meta` and are unchanged.
- **Verification:** born-dark OK across 20 images; purity 6/3 (down from 8/5 — the concurrent LIGHTER-ONLY cut); lighter_client / funding_basis / funding_carry_bot / taker `--selftest` + `--selftest-live` / funding bot / trend bot all green. 4 mutations on the new guard, all caught.
